D’Amato
et al.

[
8
]
have demonstrated that
TOB-SVD
satisfies both Safety and Liveness as defined in Definition
3
assuming
|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t-1)}}\setminus{A_{4\Delta t+2\Delta}}|>|A_{4\Delta t+2\Delta}|

17
17

17

Note that we consider the adversary at round
4\Delta t+2\Delta
due to the decision phase. This constraint differs from the one used in Constraint (
1
), which was intended for the modified version of the protocol that we will present shortly, which does not include a decision phase.
. While the original proofs by D’Amato
et al.
assume
\eta=1
, the underlying arguments can readily be extended to scenarios where
\eta>1
. This extension is feasible assuming a variant of Constraint (
1
) that considers the set
A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
as the set of adversarial validators active at round
4\Delta t+2\Delta
, and by considering only the latest messages cast by each validator. We do not directly prove this claim in the context of the current protocol. Instead, we demonstrate it in a modified version of the protocol, which we will present in the following.

The construction of a probabilistically safe total-order broadcast can be achieved by leveraging the protocol just presented, with a critical understanding that the validators’ behavior is unaffected by decisions. Decisions primarily serve as a confirmation rule for the protocol and to ensure deterministic safety. In particular, the decide phase of slot
t
encapsulates both the decision-making and the storage of
\mathcal{V}^{\prime\prime}
, which is then used in the subsequent slot for further decisions. This indicates that all activities within the decide phase are pertinent only to making decisions, and nothing else. By omitting the decide phase, the protocol can be simplified, retaining only the necessary components to maintain probabilistic safety. We have then the following modified protocol.

1.

Propose,
3\Delta t
:
If
v_{i}
is the proposer for slot
t
, send message [
propose
,
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
,
t
,
v_{i}
] through gossip, with
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{V}_{i},B_{\mathrm{genesis}},t)
and
\mathsf{ch}_{p}.p=t
.

2.

Vote,
3\Delta t+\Delta
:
Let
{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}:=\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathrm{frozen}},\mathcal{V}_{i},B_{\mathrm{genesis}},t)
and
send message [
vote
,
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}
,
\cdot,

t
,
v_{i}
] through gossip, with
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}
a
propose
d chain in slot
t
extending
{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}
and
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}.p=t
, or
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}={\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}

if no such proposal exists. Set
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}=\left({\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}\right)^{\lceil\kappa}
.

3.

3\Delta t+2\Delta
:
Set
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}=\mathcal{V}
, and store
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
. Set
t=t+1
.

It is possible to show that when there is synchrony, Constraint (
1
) (with slots of duration
3\Delta
) holds, and assuming an honest proposer for slot
t
, it is guaranteed that the
B
will extend the lock (
i.e.
,
\mathsf{ch}^{3\Delta t+\Delta}_{i}
). As a result, all honest validators of slot
t
will vote for the honest proposal
B
of slot
t
. This property ensures that the validators’ votes are consistently aligned with the honest proposal. This property allows us to obtain
\eta
Reorg Resilience. In turn,
\eta
Reorg Resilience gives us probabilistic safety for the
\kappa
-deep confirmation rule. Due to the similarity in the arguments presented in the proofs, we will demonstrate these statements on a final modification of the initially discussed protocol.

5.2
Probabilistically safe total-order broadcast with fast confirmations

This ultimate version of the protocol, presented in
Algorithm

3
, incorporates
fast confirmations
. The integration of these fast confirmations will serve as a building block for our majority-based faster finality protocol.

To this extent,
Algorithm

3
defines the function
\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V},t)
to returns the tuple
(\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q^{C})
where
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
is a chain that has garnered support from more than
\frac{2}{3}n
of the validators that have
vote
d in slot
t
according to the view
\mathcal{V}
, and
Q^{C}
, called
quorum certificate,
is a proof of this consisting of
vote
messages for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
.
If such chain does not exist, then it just returns the tuple
(B_{\mathrm{genesis}},\emptyset)
.

Also,
Algorithm

3
relies on the following external function.

•

\texttt{Extend}(\mathsf{ch},t)
:
If
\mathsf{ch}
’s length is less than
t
, then
\texttt{Extend}(\mathsf{ch},t)
produces a chain
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}
of length
t
that extends
\mathsf{ch}
and that includes all of the transactions in the transaction pool at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
.
The behavior is left unspecified for the case that
\mathsf{ch}
’s length is
t
or higher.
This will not cause us any problem because, as we will show, this case can never happen.
Formally,

Property 1
.

\displaystyle\mathsf{ch}.p<t\implies

\displaystyle\land\texttt{Extend}(\mathsf{ch},t)\succ\mathsf{ch}

\displaystyle\land\forall\mathit{tx}\in\mathit{txpool}^{\mathsf{propose}(t)},\mathit{tx}\in\texttt{Extend}(\mathsf{ch},t)

Moreover, as per
Algorithm

3
, each honest validator
v_{i}
maintains the following state variables:

•

Message Set
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
: Each validator stores in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
a snapshot of its view
\mathcal{V}_{i}
at time
3\Delta
in each slot.

•

Chain variable
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
: Each validator also stores in
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}
the greatest fast confirmed chain at time
3\Delta
in each slot
t
.
The variable
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
is then updated between time
0
and
\Delta
of slot
t+1
incorporating the information received via the
propose
message for slot
t+1
.
This can be seen as effectively executing the view-merge logic (largely employed in the protocol presented in
Section

6
) on this variable.

Additionally,
Algorithm

3
outputs the following chain.

•

Confirmed Chain
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}
:
The confirmed chain
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}
is updated at time
\Delta
in each slot and also potentially at time
2\Delta
.

In the remainder of this work, when we want to refer to the value of a variable
\mathsf{var}

after
the execution of the code for round
r
, we write
\mathsf{var}^{r}
.
If the variable already has a superscript, like
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
, we simply write
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen},r}
.

Then,
Algorithm

3
proceeds as follows.

Propose:

At round
4\Delta t
, there is the propose phase.
Here, the proposer
v_{p}
of slot
t
broadcasts the proposal
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q^{C},\cdot,t,v_{p}]
.
Here,
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
is the chain returned by the function
\texttt{Extend}({\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}},t)
where
{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}
corresponds to the output of the fork-choice
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{V}_{i},B_{\mathrm{genesis}},t)
.
Whereas,
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
and
Q^{C}
are the fast confirmed chain and relative quorum certificate, respectively, according to the current view of
v_{p}
as per output of
fastconfirmsimple
. Note that the proposer inputs in the fork-choice function
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
the current view
\mathcal{V}_{p}
and fast confirmed chain
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
.

Vote:

During the interval
[4\Delta t,4\Delta t+\Delta]
, a validator
v_{i}
, upon receiving a proposal
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q^{C},\cdot,t^{\prime},v_{p}]
from the proposer
v_{p}
for slot
t^{\prime}=t
, first verifies whether such proposal is
valid
.
This corresponds to checking whether
Q^{C}
is a valid certificate for
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
and
\mathsf{ch}_{p}.p=t
. If the
propose
message is valid, then validator
v_{i}
updates its local variable
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}
according to the received proposal.

During the voting phase, each validator
v_{i}
computes the fork-choice function
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
to continue building upon its output.
The inputs to
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
are
v_{i}
’s view at time
3\Delta
in the previous slot (
\mathcal{V}^{4\Delta(t-1)+3\Delta}_{i}=\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}
), the current view (
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{vote}(t)}_{i}
), and the chain
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
updated after receiving the
propose
message for slot
t
, if any.

Observe that by the definition of
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
(
Algorithm

2
), when computing the fork-choice during the voting phase, validator
v_{i}
only considers chains for which it received
vote
messages by round
4\Delta(t-1)+3\Delta
, extending the chain from validators who have not equivocated up to the current round (
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
), according to
v_{i}
’s view.
In contrast, when computing the fork-choice during the propose phase, the proposer considers any chain for which it received
vote
messages by the proposing time (
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
), extending the chain from validators that have never equivocated according to the proposer’s view at that time (
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
).
This approach ensures that the chain output by the fork-choice function in the voting phase is the prefix of the chain output by the fork-choice function in the propose phase of the same slot.
This property is also known as Graded Delivery
[
8
]
.

If validator
v_{i}
receives a valid
propose
message for a chain that extends the output of the fork-choice function, it casts a
vote
for that chain. Otherwise, it casts a
vote
for the current output of the fork-choice function.

In the voting phase, validator
v_{i}
also sets the confirmed chain
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}
to the highest chain among the
\kappa
-deep prefix of the chain output by
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
and the previous confirmed chain, filtering out this last chain if it is not a prefix of the chain output by
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
.

Fast Confirm:

Validator
v_{i}
checks if a chain
\mathsf{ch}
can be fast confirmed, i.e., if at least
2/3n
of the validators cast a
vote
message for a chain
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{cand}}
. If that is the case, it sets
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}
to this chain.

Merge:

At round
4\Delta t+3\Delta
, validator
v_{i}
updates its variables
\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
and
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
to be used in the following slot.

For readability purpose, we often use
{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}:=4\Delta t+2\Delta
and
{\mathsf{merge}(t)}:=4\Delta t+3\Delta
.

4\Delta t+3\Delta

do

\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow\mathcal{V}_{i}

(\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i},\cdot)\leftarrow\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)

upon
receiving a gossiped message
[\textsc{propose},{\mathsf{ch}_{p}},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},\cdot,t,v_{p}]

at any round in

[4\Delta t,4\Delta t+\Delta]

do

if

\mathrm{valid}([\textsc{propose},{\mathsf{ch}_{p}},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},\cdot,t,v_{p}])\land\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\preceq\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}

then

\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}

5.2.1
Analysis

Now, we proceed with proving that
Algorithm

3
satisfied
\eta
Reorg Resilience (
Theorem

3
),

\eta
Dynamic Availability (
Theorem

4
),

\eta
Asynchronous Reorg Resilience (
Theorem

5
)
and
\eta
Asynchronous Safety Resilience (
Theorem

6
).
Remember that throughout this work we assume that less than one-third of the entire validator set is ever controlled by the adversary (
i.e.
,
f<\frac{n}{3}
).
In all the following results, we also assume
\mathsf{GST}=0
, , and that Constraint (
1
) holds.

In the remaining part of this section, given any slot
t
, we let

\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i}:=\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i},\mathsf{ch}^{C},t)
with

(\mathsf{ch}^{C},\cdot)=\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{propose}(t)}_{i},t-1)
, and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}:=\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},t)
.

At high level, our analysis proceeds as follows.

Lemmas

4
,
5
and
6
set the base argument.
Specifically, in
Lemma

4
, we show that if all active validators in a given slot
t

vote
for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
, then, for any validator active at the following slot
t+1
, the output of the fork-choice function
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
will be an extension of
\mathsf{ch}
which, importantly, also implies that any validator active in slot
t+1

vote
for an extension of
\mathsf{ch}
.
Then,
Lemma

5
leverages
Lemma

4
to show that if any active validator fast confirms a chain
\mathsf{ch}
in a given slot
t
, then for any validator active at any subsequent slot, the output of the fork-choice function
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
will be an extension of
\mathsf{ch}
.
Finally, in
Lemma

6
we show similar results for any chain proposed by an honest validator.
Thereafter,
Lemma

7
makes use of these initial results to prove that the chain confirmed by any validator honest in a given round
r_{i}
, regardless of whether they are active in that round, is a prefix of the chain output by the fork-choice
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
by any validator active at subsequent rounds.
This allows us to then prove
\eta
Reorg Resilience in
Theorem

3
.
Moving on,
Lemma

8
shows that the premise of
Property

1
is always satisfied which implies that honest proposers are always able to include all the transactions in the transaction pool in the chain that they propose.
This,
Lemma

6
and
Lemma

7
are then put to use in
Theorem

4
to show that
Algorithm

3
is an
\eta
-dynamically-available protocol.
Thereafter,
Lemma

9
proves that chains can be fast confirmed which is not listed among the security properties but very relevant to
Algorithm

3
.
Finally, the remaining Lemmas and Theorems deal with asynchrony resilience and we leave their high-level overview up to that point.

Lemma 4
.

If, in slot
t
, all validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
cast
vote
messages for chains extending chain
\mathsf{ch}
,
then, for any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
, which implies that, in slot
t+1
, all validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
cast
vote
messages for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}
.

Proof.

Let
v_{i}
be any honest validator in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
.
Due to the joining protocol, this implies that
v_{i}
is awake at round
{\mathsf{merge}(t)}=4\Delta(t+1)-\Delta
.
Clearly the same holds at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}
:
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\lor\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.
Now we consider each case separately and for each of them prove that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.

Case 1:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}
.

Note that due to the Lemma’s and synchrony assumptions, all the
vote
messages sent by validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
are included in
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}
.
Hence, at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}
,
\left|\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{ch},t+1}\right|\geq\left|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right|
.
Similarly,
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\subseteq\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)
.
Also,
\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)\setminus\left(H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right)\subseteq A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\cup\left(H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t-\eta+1)},{\mathsf{vote}(t-1)}}\setminus H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\right)
as any
vote
in
\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)
that is not from a validator in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
must be either from a validator Byzantine at time
{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}
or from a validator active at some point between
{\mathsf{vote}(t-\eta+1)}
and
{\mathsf{vote}(t-1)}
but not active at time
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
.
Then, we have

\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)\right|=\left|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right|+\left|\left(\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)\setminus\left(H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right)\right)\right|\leq\left|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right|+\left|A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\cup\left(H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t-\eta+1)},{\mathsf{vote}(t-1)}}\setminus H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\right)\right|<2\left|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right|
where the last inequality comes from
Equation

1
.

Hence, at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}
,
\left|\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{ch},t+1}\right|
\geq\left|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)\right|}{2}
.

Therefore,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.

Case 2:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.

Given that by definition,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}
, we have that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.

Now let us move to proving that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.
Note that if a
[\textsc{propose},B,\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C},Q^{C}_{p},t+1,v_{p}]
message is valid, then
\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\lor\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.

Algorithm

3
implies that
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t)}}_{i}\lor\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C}
.
Then, from
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t)}}_{i}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\lor\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
and
Algorithm

3
, it follows that,
at time
{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}
,
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\lor\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.
Now we consider each case separately.

Case 1:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}
.

Note that due to the Lemma’s and synchrony assumptions, all the
vote
messages sent by validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
are included in
\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
.
From here the proof is effectively the same as the one for Case 1 for
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}
, just with
\mathcal{V}_{i}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}
replaced by
\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
.

Case 2:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.

Similarly to proof of Case 2 for
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}
, given that by definition,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}
, we have that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.l

From
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
follows that, in slot
t+1
,
v_{i}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.
∎

Lemma 5
.

If an honest validator fast confirms a chain
\mathsf{ch}
in slot
t
(
\emph{i.e.}
, there exists
Q
such that
(\mathsf{ch},Q)=\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}}_{i},t)\land Q\neq\emptyset
), then, for any slot
t^{\prime}>t
and validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime})}}
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t^{\prime})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
, which implies that, all validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime})}}
cast a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
t^{\prime}
.

Base Case:
t^{\prime}=t+1
.

Assume that at round
{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}
, an honest validator in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
fast confirms a chain
\mathsf{ch}
.
Given that we assume
f<\frac{n}{3}
, conflicting quorum certificates cannot form in the same slot.
Then, due to the joining protocol and the synchrony assumption, at time
{\mathsf{merge}(t)}=4\Delta(t+1)-\Delta
,
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen, {$\mathsf{merge}(t)$}}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}
for any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
.

Similarly, if a
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C},Q^{C}_{p},t+1,v_{p}]
message is valid, then
\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C}=B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\lor\mathsf{ch}_{p}^{C}=\mathsf{ch}
.
This and
3
to
3
imply that, at time
{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}
,
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}
.

Hence, because
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
, implying that all validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
cast
vote
messages extending chain
\mathsf{ch}
.

Inductive Step:
t^{\prime}>t+1.

Here we can just apply
Lemma

4
to conclude the proof.∎

Lemma 6
.

Let
t
be a slot with an honest proposer
v_{p}
and assume that
v_{p}
casts a
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},t,v_{p}]
message.
Then, for any slot
t^{\prime}\geq t
, all validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime})}}
cast a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.
Additionally, for any slot
t^{\prime\prime}>t
and any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime\prime})}}
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t^{\prime\prime})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime\prime})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
t^{\prime}
.

Base Case:
t^{\prime}=t
.

Suppose that in slot
t
, an honest proposer
v_{p}
sends a
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p}t,v_{p}]
message.
Consider an honest validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
.
Note that due to the synchrony assumption,
\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{p}
.
Given that we assume
f<\frac{n}{3}
, this further implies that
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}\neq B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\implies\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}=\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
.
Hence, clearly
\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
.
Therefore, due to
3
to
3
,
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.

We know that either
\left|\left(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}\right)^{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},t}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},t}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)\right|}{2}
or
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}
.
Let us consider each case separately.

Case 1:
\left|\left(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}\right)^{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},t}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},t}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)\right|}{2}
.

By the Graded Delivery property
[
8
]
, this implies that at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
,
\left|\mathcal{V}_{p}^{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i},t}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{p},t)\right|}{2}
meaning that, due to
Algorithm

3
,
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}
and hence, due to
3
and
3
, in slot
t
,
v_{i}
casts a
vote
message for
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.

Case 2:
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}
.

Due to
3
and
3
,
v_{i}
still casts a
vote
for
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
as
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}=\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}=\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}
.

Inductive Step:
t^{\prime}>t
.

Here we can just apply
Lemma

4
to conclude the proof.∎

Lemma 7
.

Let
r_{i}
be any round and
r_{j}
be any round such that
r_{j}\geq r_{i}
and
r_{j}\in\{{\mathsf{propose}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j}))},{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j}))}\}
. Then, for any validator
v_{i}
honest in round
r_{i}
and any validator
v_{j}\in H_{r_{j}}
,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\preceq\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{r_{j}}_{j}
.

Proof.

We proceed by contradiction.
Let
r_{i}
be the smallest round such that there exist two honest validators
v_{i}
and
v_{j}
, and round
r_{j}
such that
r_{j}\geq r_{i}
and
r_{j}\in\{{\mathsf{propose}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j}))},{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j}))}\}
and

\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\npreceq\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{r_{j}}_{j}
,
that is,
r_{i}
is the first round where the chain confirmed by an honest validator conflicts with the output of
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
of (another) honest validator at a propose or vote round
r_{j}\geq r_{i}
.
Given the minimality of
r_{i}
,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}-1}_{i}\neq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
which then implies that
v_{i}\in H_{r_{i}}
.
This can only happen if
r_{i}
is either a voting or a fast confirmation round.
Let
t_{i}=\mathrm{slot}(r_{i})
and proceed by analyzing each case separately.

Case 1:
r_{i}
is a vote round.

Due to
Algorithm

3
,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\succeq\left(\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\right)^{\lceil\kappa}
.
Let us now consider two sub cases.

Case 1.1:
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}=\left(\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\right)^{\lceil\kappa}
.

With overwhelming probability (Lemma 2
[
10
]
), there exists at least one slot
t_{p}
in the interval
[t_{i}-\kappa,t_{i})
with an honest proposer
v_{p}
.
Let
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
be the chain
propose
d by
v_{p}
in slot
t_{p}
.
Given that
t_{p}<t_{i}
,
Lemma

6
implies that

\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.
Then, because
t_{p}\geq t_{i}-\kappa
, we have that
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\left(\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\right)^{\lceil\kappa}=\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
Because
t_{p}<\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j})
,
Lemma

6
also implies that
\mathsf{ch}^{r_{j}}_{j}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
leading to a contradiction.

Case 1.2:
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\succ\left(\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\right)^{\lceil\kappa}
.

This case implies that
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}=\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}-1}_{i}
.
From the minimality of
r_{i}
we reach a contradiction.

Case 2:
r_{i}
is a fast confirmation round.

Note that this implies that
t_{i}<\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j})
.
Because of the minimality of
r_{i}
, we only need to consider the case that
(\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i},Q)=\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V}^{r_{i}}_{i},t_{i})\land Q\neq\emptyset
.
Therefore, we can apply
Lemma

5
to conclude that
\mathsf{ch}^{r_{j}}_{j}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
reaching a contradiction.∎

Theorem 3

(Reorg Resilience)
.

Algorithm

3
is
\eta
-reorg-resilient.

Proof.

Take a slot
t_{p}
with an honest proposer
v_{p}
who sends a
propose
message for chain
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.
Take also any round
r_{i}
and validator
v_{i}
honest in round
r_{i}
.
Now let
t_{j}
be any slot such that
t_{j}>\max(t_{p},\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))
.
By Constraint (
2
) we know that
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}
is not empty.
So, pick any validator
v_{j}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}
.

Lemma

6
implies that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}_{j}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.

Lemma

7
implies that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}_{j}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
Hence,
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
does not conflict with
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
∎

Lemma 8
.

For any slot
t
and validator
v_{i}\in H_{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i}.p<t
.

Proof.

Due to the Validity property of Graded Agreement
[
8
]
and the fact that
B_{\mathrm{genesis}}.p<0
,
it is sufficient to prove that no validator honest in round
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
has ever sent a
vote
message for a chain
\mathsf{ch}
with
\mathsf{ch}.p\geq t
.
The proof is by induction.

Base Case:
t=0
.

Obvious as no validator in
H_{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
has ever sent any message.

Inductive Step:
t>0
.

Due to the inductive hypothesis and the fact that honest nodes do not send any
vote
message in round
{\mathsf{propose}(t-1)}
, by the Validity property of Graded Agreement, we know that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t-1)}}.p<t-1
.
Then, the proof follows from
Algorithm

3
.∎

Theorem 4
.

Algorithm

3
is
\eta
-dynamically-available.

Proof.

We begin by proving the
\eta
Liveness of the protocol with a confirmation time of
T_{\text{conf}}=8\kappa\Delta+\Delta
.
We prove liveness by first considering the
\kappa
-deep rule only.
Take a round
r
at slot
t=\text{slot}(r)
, another round
r_{i}\geq r+8\kappa\Delta+\Delta\geq 4\Delta(t+2\kappa)+\Delta={\mathsf{vote}(t+2\kappa)}
, and an honest validator
v_{i}\in H_{r_{i}}
.
Let
t_{i}=\mathrm{slot}(r_{i})
.
Due to the joining protocol, we know that the first active round for
v_{i}
, at or after
{\mathsf{vote}(t+2\kappa)}
, is a vote round.
There is a high probability of finding a slot
t_{p}\in[t+1,t+\kappa]
hosted by an honest proposer (Lemma 2
[
10
]
).
Let
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
be the chain
propose
d by the honest proposer
v_{p}
in slot
t_{p}
.
Due to
Lemma

8
and
Property

1
, we know that
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
includes all of the transaction in
\mathit{txpool}^{\mathsf{propose}(t_{p})}
.
Given that
{\mathsf{propose}(t_{p})}\geq r
,
\mathit{txpool}^{r}\subseteq\mathit{txpool}^{\mathsf{propose}(t_{p})}
, which implies that
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
includes all of the transactions in
\mathit{txpool}^{r}
.

Given that
\mathrm{slot}(r_{i})>t_{p}
, as a consequence of
Lemma

6
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.
Note that
Algorithm

3
implies that
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\succeq\left(\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\right)^{\lceil\kappa,t_{i}}
.
Then, because
t_{p}\leq t+\kappa\leq t_{i}-\kappa
,
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\preceq\left(\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\right)^{\lceil\kappa,t_{i}}
and hence
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\preceq\mathsf{Ch}_{i}^{r_{i}}
, which implies that
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}^{r_{i}}
includes any transaction in
\mathit{txpool}^{r}
.

We now want to show that fast confirmation does not interfere.
Given that
t_{i}\geq t_{j}
, from
Lemma

6
, we know that any
vote
cast in slot
t_{i}
by honest validators are for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
, given that we assume
f<\frac{n}{3}
, if
r_{i}
is a fast confirmation round and
v_{i}
sets
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
, then
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
still holds.

We now show
\eta-
safety.
Take any two rounds
r_{i}
and
r_{j}
and validators
v_{i}
and
v_{j}
honest in round
r_{i}
and
r_{j}
respectively.
Now let
t_{k}
be any slot such that
t_{k}>\max(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{j}),\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))
.
By Constraint (
1
) we know that
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}
is not empty.
So, pick any validator
v_{k}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}
.

Lemma

7
implies that
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{j}}_{j}
.
Hence,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
does not conflict with
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{j}}_{j}
.
∎

Lemma 9

(Liveness of fast confirmations)
.

Take a slot
t
in which
|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}|\geq\frac{2}{3}n
.
If in slot
t
an honest validator sends a
propose
message for chain
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
, then, for any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}}
,
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.

Proof.

By assumption we have that
|H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}|\geq\frac{2}{3}n
, implying from
Lemma

6
that every honest validator in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
casts a
vote
message for
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
. It follows that any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}}
receives a quorum of
vote
messages for
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.
Hence, due to
3
and
3
,
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.
∎

Finally, we move to
demonstrating that
Algorithm

3
also provides
\eta
Asynchrony Reorg Resilience and
\eta
Asynchrony Safety Resilience, as defined in Section
2.3
.

Lemma

10
shows that if from slot
t_{a}
till a slot
t^{\prime}
falling within the short period of asynchrony, all aware validators
vote
for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}
, then so will do all aware validators at the next slot
t^{\prime}+1
.
Next,
Lemma

12
leverages this result proving that, if in a slot before the short period of asynchrony starts, all active validators
vote
for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}
, then the chain confirmed by any aware validator at any subsequent round does not conflict with
\mathsf{ch}
.
This and
Theorem

3
allow then concluding in
Theorem

5
that
Algorithm

3
is
\eta
-asynchrony-reorg-resilient.
Finally,
Theorem

6
makes use of
Lemmas

7
and
12
, and
Theorem

4
to prove that
Algorithm

3
is also
\eta
-asynchrony-safety-resilient.

Lemma 10
.

Let
t
be any slot in
[t_{a},t_{a}+\pi]
with
\pi>0
.
Assume that in any slot
t^{\prime}\in[t_{a},t]
, all validators in
W_{{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime})}}
cast
vote
messages for chains extending chain
\mathsf{ch}
.
Then, in slot
t+1
, all validators in
W_{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}
also cast
vote
messages for chains extending chain
\mathsf{ch}
.

Proof.

The lemma makes use of two main ingredients: the expiration period is
\eta=\pi+2
, and Constraint (
2
). Firstly, the votes which influence the voting phase in slot
t+1
are those from slots
[t-\eta,t]
.
These include votes from slot
t_{a}
since
t+1-\eta\leq t_{a}+\pi+1-\eta<t_{a}
. Since by
Equation

3

all validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+1)}}
were awake at
4\Delta t_{a}+2\Delta
, they have all received each other’s votes from slot
t_{a}
.
Then, from the Lemma’s assumption, for any validator
v_{i}\in W_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
and any validator
v_{a}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
, the latest message from
v_{a}
in
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}_{i}
is for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.
By Constraint (
2
), validators in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}
constitute a majority of all unexpired votes in slot
t+1
. This implies that any such validator
v_{i}
would again have
\left|\left(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}\right)^{\mathsf{ch},t+1}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{ch},t+1}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t+1)\right|}{2}
.
Note also that because we assume
f<\frac{n}{3}
, for any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}}
, if
(\mathsf{ch}^{\prime},Q)=\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{fconf}(t)}_{i},t)
and
Q\neq\emptyset
, then
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.
Therefore, any such validator would in slot
t+1
cast a
vote
for an extension of
\mathsf{ch}
.
∎

Lemma 11
.

Assume
\pi>0
and
take a slot
t\leq t_{a}
such that, in slot
t
, any validator in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.
Then, for any slot
t_{i}\geq t
, any validator in
W_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
t_{i}
.

Base Case:
t_{i}\in[t,t_{a}]
.

From
Lemma

4
.

Inductive Step:
t_{i}>t_{a}
.

We assume that the Lemma holds for slot
t_{i}-1
and prove that it holds also for slot
t_{i}
.
Let us proceed by cases.

Case 1:
t_{i}\in[t_{a}+1,t_{a}+\pi+1]
.

From
Lemma

10
.

Case 2:
t_{i}=t_{a}+\pi+2
.

From the induction hypothesis, we know that in slot
t_{a}+\pi+1
, any validator in
W_{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+1)}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.
Since slot
t_{a}+\pi+1
is synchronous, such
vote
messages are all in the view
\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+2)}}_{i}
of any validator
v_{i}\in W_{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+2)}=H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+2)}}
.
Also, given that
\eta=\pi+2
, such
vote
messages are not expired in slot
t_{a}+\pi+2
as
t_{a}+\pi+2-\eta\leq t_{a}
.
Given that Constraint (
2
) holds for
t_{a}+\pi+2
, we can use the same reasoning applied in
Lemma

10
to conclude that validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+2)}}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.

Case 3:
t_{a}\geq t_{a}+\pi+3
.

Given that for any slot
t_{j}\geq t_{a}+\pi+2
,
W_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}=H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}
, here we can just apply
Lemma

4
.∎

Lemma 12
.

Assume
\pi>0
and
take a slot
t\leq t_{a}
such that, in slot
t
, any validator in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{ch}
.
Then, for any round
r_{i}\geq{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
and validator
v_{i}\in W_{r_{i}}
,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
does not conflict with
\mathsf{ch}
.

Proof.

Take any round
r_{i}\geq{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
and validator
v_{i}\in W_{r_{i}}
.
Given that the confirmed chain is updated only either in a voting or fast confirmation round, let us consider the two following cases.

Case 1:
r_{i}={\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}
.

From
Lemma

11
we know that any validator
v_{i}\in W_{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}
casts a
vote
messages for some chain
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
.
Due to
3
to
3
,
\mathsf{ch}^{\prime}\succeq\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
Hence,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
does not conflict with
\mathsf{ch}
.

Case 2:
r_{i}={\mathsf{fconf}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}
.

Let us consider two sub cases.

Case 2.1:
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}=\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}-1}_{i}
.

This implies that
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}=\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}}_{i}
.
Due to the joining protocol,
v_{i}\in W_{{\mathsf{fconf}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}}
implies
v_{i}\in W_{{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}}
.
Therefore, this case is proved by Case 1 above.

Case 2.2:
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\neq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}-1}_{i}
.

This implies
(\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i},Q)=\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V}^{r_{i}}_{i},\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))\land Q\neq\emptyset
.
Given that, as established above, all validators in
W_{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}))}
cast
vote
messages for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}
, Constraint (
2
) implies that the number of validators that might cast a
vote
message for a chain non extending
\mathsf{ch}
is less than half the total number of validators.
This implies that any chain non extending
\mathsf{ch}
cannot receive a quorum of
vote
message for it.
Therefore,
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
meaning that
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
does not conflict with
\mathsf{ch}
.∎

Theorem 5

(Asynchrony Reorg Resilience)
.

Algorithm

3
is
\eta
-asynchrony-reorg-resilient.

Proof.

Assume
\pi>0
and
take a slot
t_{p}\leq t_{a}
with an honest proposer, any round
r_{i}
and any validator
v_{i}\in W_{r_{i}}
.
If
r_{i}<{\mathsf{vote}(t_{p})}
, we can apply
Theorem

3
.
Otherwise, we can apply
Lemmas

6
and
12
.
∎

Theorem 6

(Asynchrony Safety Resilience)
.

Algorithm

3

\eta
-asynchrony-safety-resilient.

Proof.

Assume
\pi>0
and
pick any round
r_{i}\leq 4\Delta t_{a}+\Delta
, any round
r_{j}
, any validator
v_{i}
honest in
r_{i}
and any validator
v_{j}\in W_{r_{j}}
.
Let us now proceed by cases.

Case 1:
r_{j}<{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}
.

The proof for this case follows from
\eta
Safety (
Theorem

4
).

Case 2:
r_{j}\geq{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}
.

Lemma

7
implies that for any validator
v_{k}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}
,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}_{k}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
This implies that, in slot
t_{a}
,
v_{k}
casts a
vote
message for a chain extending
\mathsf{Ch}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
Hence, we can apply
Lemma

12
to conclude the proof for this case.∎

We are now prepared to develop our faster-finality protocol, which integrates the dynamically-available protocol
Algorithm

3
with the FFG components introduced in
Section

4
to obtain an
\eta
-secure ebb-and-flow protocol.

5.3
Faster finality protocol execution

Algorithm 4

Faster finality protocol – code for validator
v_{i}

Output

\mathsf{chAva}_{i}\leftarrow B_{\mathrm{genesis}}
: available chain

\mathsf{chFin}_{i}\leftarrow B_{\mathrm{genesis}}
: finalized chain

State

\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\text{frozen}}\leftarrow\{B_{\mathrm{genesis}}\}
: snapshot of
\mathcal{V}
at time
4\Delta t+3\Delta

\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow B_{\mathrm{genesis}}
: snapshot of the fast confirmed chain at time
4\Delta t+3\Delta

\mathcal{GJ}_{i}^{\text{frozen}}\leftarrow(B_{\mathrm{genesis}},0)
: latest frozen greatest justified checkpoint

function

\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V},t)

let

(\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q):=\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V},t)

if

\mathsf{ch}^{C}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}).\mathsf{ch}

then

return

(\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q)

else

return

(\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}).\mathsf{ch},\emptyset)

Propose

at round

4\Delta t

do

if

v_{i}=v_{p}^{t}

then

let

(\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q^{C}):=\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t-1)

let

{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}:=\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathsf{ch}^{C},t)

let

\mathsf{ch}_{p}:={\mathsf{Extend}({\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}},t)}

send message [
propose
,
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
,
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
,
Q^{C}
,
\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}_{i})
,
t
,
v_{i}
] through gossip

Vote

at round

4\Delta t+\Delta

do

let

{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}:=\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i},\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i},t)

\mathsf{chAva}_{i}\leftarrow\max(\{\mathsf{ch}\in\{\mathsf{chAva}_{i},{({\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}})^{\lceil\kappa}},\mathcal{GJ}_{i}^{\text{frozen}}.\mathsf{ch}\}:\mathsf{ch}\preceq{{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}}\})

\mathsf{chFin}_{i}\leftarrow\max(\{\mathsf{ch}\colon\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathsf{chAva}_{i}\land\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathcal{GF}(\mathcal{V}_{i}).\mathsf{ch}\})

let

\mathcal{T}:=(\mathsf{chAva}_{i},t)

let

\mathsf{ch}:=

propose
d chain from slot
t
extending
{{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}}
and with
\mathsf{ch}.p=t
, if there is one, or
{{\mathsf{ch}^{\mathsf{MFC}}}}
otherwise

send message [
vote
,
\mathsf{ch}
,
\mathcal{GJ}_{i}^{\text{frozen}}\to\mathcal{T}
,
t
,
v_{i}
] through gossip

Fast Confirm

at round

4\Delta t+2\Delta

do

let

(\mathsf{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}},\cdot):=\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)

if

\mathsf{chAva}_{i}\nsucceq\mathsf{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}}

then

\mathsf{chAva}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}}

\mathsf{chFin}_{i}\leftarrow\mathcal{GF}(\mathcal{V}_{i}).\mathsf{ch}

Merge

at round

4\Delta t+3\Delta

do

\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow\mathcal{V}_{i}

(\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i},\cdot)\leftarrow\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)

\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}_{i})

upon
receiving a gossiped message

[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},\mathcal{GJ}_{p},t,v_{p}]

at any round in

[4\Delta t,4\Delta t+\Delta]

do

when round

4\Delta t+\Delta

do

if

\text{valid}([\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},\mathcal{GJ}_{p},t,v_{p}])\land\mathsf{J}(\mathcal{GJ}_{p},\mathcal{V}_{i})\land\mathcal{GJ}_{p}\geq\mathcal{GJ}_{i}^{\text{frozen}}

then

\mathcal{GJ}_{i}^{\text{frozen}}\leftarrow\mathcal{GJ}_{p}

if

\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\not\succeq\mathcal{GJ}_{p}.\mathsf{ch}

then

\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow\mathcal{GJ}_{p}.\mathsf{ch}

if

\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\preceq\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}

then

\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}\leftarrow\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}

In this section, we present
Algorithm

4
which integrates the
\eta
-dynamically-available and reorg-resilient protocol of
Algorithm

3
with the FFG component introduced in
Section

4
to obtain a
\eta
-secure ebb-and-flow protocol.
We describe
Algorithm

4
by discussing the main differences compared to
Algorithm

3
.

The first difference is in the logic employed by
Algorithm

4
to determine fast confirmed chains.
This is encoded in the function
\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V},t)
which returns the same output of
\texttt{fastconfirmsimple}(\mathcal{V},t)
from
Algorithm

4
as long as the returned fast confirmed chain extends
\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}).\mathsf{ch}
.
Otherwise, it just returns
(\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}).\mathsf{ch},\emptyset)
.
The reason underpinning this change is to allow
\eta
-dynamical-availability and Reorg Resilience to be ensured again after long periods of asynchrony once the network becomes synchronous (see
Section

8
for more details).

Second, compared to
Algorithm

3
,
Algorithm

4
maintains the following additional state variable:

•

Checkpoint Variable
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
: Each validator stores in
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
the greatest justified checkpoint according to their view at time
3\Delta
in slot
t-1
.
Like
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
, this variable is then updated between time
0
and
\Delta
in slot
t
incorporating the information received via the
propose
message for slot
t
.
Also like
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
, this can be seen as effectively executing a view-merge on this variable.

Next,
Algorithm

4
outputs two chains.

•

Available Chain
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
:
This roughly corresponds to the confirmed chain
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}
of
Algorithm

3
.

•

Finalized Chain
\mathsf{chFin}_{i}
:

At any fast confirmation round,
\mathsf{chFin}
is set to
\mathcal{GF}(\mathcal{V}_{i}).\mathsf{ch}
,
i.e.
, the chain of the greatest justified checkpoint.

Also, at any vote round, the finalized chain
\mathsf{chFin}_{i}
is set to
the longest chain such that of
\mathsf{chFin}_{i}\preceq\mathcal{GF}(\mathcal{V}_{i}).\mathsf{ch}\land\mathsf{chFin}_{i}\preceq\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
18
18

18

This is to ensure that
\mathsf{chFin}_{i}
is always a prefix of
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
, as required by
\eta
-secure ebb-and-flow protocols.
Simply, setting
\mathsf{chFin}_{i}
to
\mathcal{GF}(\mathcal{V}_{i})
in vote rounds would not ensure such properties during asynchrony.
19
19

19

Setting
\mathsf{chFin}_{i}
in vote rounds is not strictly required to ensure any of the theorems or lemmas of this work, even though, if one did remove setting
\mathsf{chFin}
in vote rounds, then one would need to tweak some of the proofs.
However, we decided to also set
\mathsf{chFin}
in vote round as, there are scenarios under asynchrony whereby setting
\mathsf{chFin}
in the vote round of slot
t
results in a chain longer than the chain
\mathsf{chFin}
that was set in the fast confirmation round of slot
t-1
.
Also, we believe, but have no formal proof for it yet, that updating
\mathsf{chFin}
at vote and fast confirmation rounds provides the highest frequency at which
\mathsf{chFin}
can be updated without breaking any of the theorems and lemmas of this work.

.

Finally, compared to
Algorithm

3
,
Algorithm

4
proceeds as follows.

Propose:

In the propose phase, the proposer for slot
t
includes in the
propose
message that it sends also the greatest justified checkpoint according to its view at the time of proposing.

Vote:

The validity condition of a
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q^{C},\mathcal{GJ}_{p},t,v_{p}]
message received by an honest validator
v_{i}
is extended to also check that
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
is indeed a justified checkpoint according to the
v_{i}
’s current view.
To give enough time for
ffg-vote
s that justify
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
to be received by
v_{i}
,
v_{i}
postpones to time
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
acting upon any
propose
messages received in the interval
[4\Delta t,4\Delta t+\Delta]
.
Then, only if at that time the validity condition passes and
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
is greater than
v_{i}
’s frozen latest greatest justified checkpoint
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
, then
v_{i}
updates its local variables
\mathcal{GJ}^{\text{frozen}}
and
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}
according with the received proposal always ensuring that
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
.

In
Algorithm

4
, honest validators also include an
ffg-vote
in the
vote
messages that they send.
Specifically, the source checkpoint of such
ffg-vote
sent corresponds to
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
.
The target checkpoint is determined by selecting the highest chain among
the previous available chain, the
\kappa
-deep prefix of the chain output by the fork-choice function
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
, and
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
, filtering out any chain if it is not a prefix of the chain output by the fork-choice function
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
.

Validator
v_{i}
also sets the available chain output,
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
to such chain.
So, compared to the confirmed chain in
Algorithm

3
, when setting
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
,
Algorithm

4
weighs the previous available chain also against
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
20
20

20

While, this change is not strictly required for correctness, under some conditions it ends up producing a longer available chain which is better from a responsiveness point of view.
For example, this is the case if we have a slot
t^{\prime}
where the proposer is honest,
\frac{2}{3}
of the validators are active and honest up to the voting phase of slot
t^{\prime}+1
, but then some of these are corrupted immediately after, and
v_{i}
is asleep in both slots and it becomes active in slot
t^{\prime}+2
only.
In such a scenario, with the modification discussed in this paragraph, in the voting phase of slot
t^{\prime}+2
,
v_{i}
sets its available chain to to the chain
propose
d by the proposer of slot
t^{\prime}
.
Without such modification,
v_{i}
’s would not update its available chain during the voting phase of slot
t^{\prime}+2
.
.

Fast Confirm:

Validator
v_{i}
checks whether
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
is a prefix of the chain
\mathrm{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}}
output by
\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)
or it conflicts with it.
In either case,
v_{i}
updates
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
to
\mathrm{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}}
21
21

21

It is possible to design an algorithm where, if
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
conflicts with
\mathsf{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}}
, then it is left to the specific implementation decide whether to update
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}
to
\mathsf{fast}^{\mathrm{cand}}
or leave it unchanged. However, to keep the algorithm straightforward, we chose not to incorporate this additional decision layer.
.

Merge:

At round
4\Delta t+3\Delta
, every validator
v_{i}
also updates
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen}}_{i}
to the greatest justified checkpoint according to its view at that time.

5.4
Faster finality protocol analysis

Algorithm

4
works in the generalized partially synchronous sleepy model, and is in particular a
\eta
-secure ebb-and-flow protocol.
In
Section

4.1
we have already shown that the the finalized chain
\mathsf{chFin}
is
\frac{n}{3}
-accountable, and thus always safe if
f<\frac{n}{3}
.
For
\mathsf{GST}=0
, we show in Section
5.4.1
that, if the execution is
\eta
-compliant in this stronger sense, then all the properties of
Algorithm

3
, i.e.,
\eta
Dynamic Availability and
\eta
Reorg Resilience, keep holding.
Finally, in
Section

5.4.2
we show that, after
\max(\mathsf{GST},\mathsf{GAT})+4\Delta
,
Algorithm

4
guarantees the required conditions listed in Property
C
to ensure that the finalized chain is live.

5.4.1
Synchrony

In this section, we prove that chain

\mathsf{chAva}
of
Algorithm

4
is
\eta
-dynamically-available,
\eta
-reorg-resilient,
\eta
-asynchrony-reorg-resilient and
\eta
-asynchrony-safety-resilient.
Throughout this part of the analysis, we assume
\mathsf{GST}=0
, that less than one-third of the entire validator set is ever controlled by the adversary (
i.e.
,
f<\frac{n}{3}
), and that Constraint (
1
) holds.
At a high level, our proof strategy is to show that, under these assumptions, the integration of the FFG component into
Algorithm

3
does not affect the protocol behavior in any way that could compromise any of the properties already proven for
Algorithm

3
.

To do so, we take any execution of
Algorithm

4
and show that there exists an adversary that induces an execution of
Algorithm

3
where (i) the messages sent by any honest validator in the two executions match except only for the FFG component of messages, (ii) the
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
fork-choice outputs of any honest validator in the two executions match and (iii) any available chain output by
Algorithm

4
is also a confirmed chain of an honest validator in the execution of
Algorithm

3
.
This then allows us to show that the result of the various Lemmas and Theorems presented in
Section

5.2.1
for
Algorithm

3
also hold for
Algorithm

4
.

We start by formalizing the concept of validators sending the same messages except only for their FFG component.
Moreover, we define the equivalence between an execution in
Algorithm

3
and one in
Algorithm

4
.
These definitions will be leveraged upon in the subsequent Lemmas and Theorems.

Definition 10
.

We say that two messages are
dynamically-equivalent
if and only if they differ in at most their FFG-related components,
i.e.
, they either are
propose
messages and differ at most in the greatest justified checkpoint component or they are
vote
messages and differ at most in the
ffg-vote
component.

We say that two sets of messages are dynamically-equivalent if and only if they have the same set of equivalence classes under type-equivalence,
i.e.
,
for any message
m
in any of the two sets, there exists a message
m^{\prime}
in the other set such that
m
and
m^{\prime}
are dynamically-equivalent.

Given two executions
e
and
e^{\prime}
and round
r
, we say that the two executions are
honest-output-dynamically-equivalent up to round
r
if and only if for any round
r_{j}<r
and validator
v_{j}
honest in round
r_{j}
in both executions, the set of messages sent by
v_{j}
in round
r_{j}
in execution
e
is dynamically-equivalent to the set of messages sent by
v_{j}
in round
r_{j}
in execution
e^{\prime}
.

In the remainder of this section, we use the notation
\overset{e}{\mathcal{X}}{}
, where
\mathcal{X}
is any variable or definition,
to explicitly indicate the value of
\mathcal{X}
in execution
e
.
Due the difference in the fast confirmation function employed by the two algorithms, we do need to explicitly specify the meaning of
\overset{e}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i}
with
t
being any slot.
If
e
is an execution of
Algorithm

3
, then
\overset{e}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i}
corresponds the definition provided in
Section

5.2.1
.
If
e
is an execution of
Algorithm

4
, then
\overset{e}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i}:=\overset{e}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}(\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{i},\mathsf{ch}^{C},t)
with
(\mathsf{ch}^{C},\cdot)=\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{propose}(t)}_{i},t-1)
.

Definition 11
.

Let
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
by an
\eta
-compliant execution of
Algorithm

4
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
be an
\eta
-compliant execution of
Algorithm

3
.
We say that
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
-equivalent
if and only if the following constraints hold:

1.

{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are honest-output-dynamically-equivalent up to any round

2.

for any slot
t_{i}
,

2.1

\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i}

2.2

\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}

2.3

for any slot
t_{j}\leq t_{i}
and validator
v_{j}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}
, there exists a slot
t_{k}\leq t_{j}
and a validator
v_{k}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}
such that
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}_{j}
.

2.4

for any slot
t_{j}\leq t_{i}
and validator
v_{j}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{j})}}
, there exists a slot
t_{k}\leq t_{j}
and a validator
v_{k}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}
such that
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{j})}}_{j}
.

where chain
\mathsf{chAva}_{j}
is in
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
, and chain
\mathsf{Ch}_{i}
is in
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
by any
\eta
-compliant execution of
Algorithm

4
.
There exists an
\eta
-compliant execution
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
of
Algorithm

3

such that
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}
-equivalent.

Proof.

Let
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
be any
\eta
-compliant execution of
Algorithm

4
and let
A^{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}
be the adversary in such an execution.
Below, we specify the decisions made by an adversary
A^{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}
in an execution
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
of
Algorithm

3
.
Later we show that this leads to
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
satisfying all the conditions in the Lemma.

(i)

For any round
r
, the set of validators corrupted by
A^{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}
in round
r
corresponds to set of validators corrupted by
A^{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}
in the same round
r
.

(ii)

For any round
r
, the set of validators put to sleep by
A^{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}
in round
r
corresponds to set of validators put to sleep by
A^{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}
in the same round
r
.

(iii)

For any slot
t
, if an honest validator is the proposer for slot
t
in
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
, then it is also the proposer for slot
t
in
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
.

(iv)

For any round
r
, if
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are honest-output-dynamically-equivalent up to round
r
, then
A^{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}
schedules the delivery of messages to the validators honest in round
r
such that, for any validator
v_{i}
honest in round
r_{i}
, the set of messages received by
v_{i}
in execution
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
is dynamically-equivalent to the set of messages received by
v_{i}
in execution
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
.

Now, we prove by induction on
t_{i}
that the execution
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
induced by
A^{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}
satisfies all
Definition

11
’s conditions.
To do so, we add the following conditions to the inductively hypothesis.

2.5

For any
\mathcal{J}
such that
\mathsf{J}(\mathcal{J},\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i})
,
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i}\succeq\mathcal{J}.\mathsf{ch}
.

2.6

For any
\mathcal{J}
such that
\mathsf{J}(\mathcal{J},\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i})
,
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\succeq\mathcal{J}.\mathsf{ch}
.

and rephrase
Item

1
as follows

2.7

{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are honest-output-dynamically-equivalent up to round
4\Delta(t_{i}+1)
.

Note that
Item

22.7
holding for any
t_{i}
implies
Item

1
.

To reduce repetitions in the proof, we treat the base case of the induction within the inductive step.

Then, take any
t_{i}\geq 0
and assume that, if
t_{i}>0
, then the Lemma and the additional conditions
Items

22.5
,
22.6
and
22.7
hold for slot
t_{i}-1
.
We prove that they also hold for slot
t_{i}
.
We start by proving
Items

22.5
and
22.6
for slot
t_{i}
,
then we move to
Items

22.1
,
22.2
,
22.3
and
22.4
in this order and conclude with proving
Item

22.7
.

Items

22.5
and
22.6
.

Let
\mathcal{J}
be any checkpoint such that
\mathsf{J}(\mathcal{J},\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i})
.
Because the chain of the target checkpoint of an
ffg-vote
cast by an honest validator
v_{\ell}
in round
r_{\ell}
corresponds to
(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathsf{chAva}}{}{}^{r_{\ell}}_{\ell}.\mathsf{ch},\cdot)
and we assume
f<\frac{n}{3}
,
there exists a slot
t_{k}\in[0,t_{i})
and a validator
v_{k}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}
such that
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathcal{J}.\mathsf{ch}
.
By the inductive hypothesis,
Item

22.3
implies that there exists a slot
t_{m}\in[0,t_{k}]
and validator
v_{m}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}
such that
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}_{m}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}
.
Given that
t_{m}\leq t_{k}<t_{i}
, from
Lemma

7
, we know that
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}_{m}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathcal{J}.\mathsf{ch}
and that
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}_{m}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathcal{J}.\mathsf{ch}
.
Given that the set of justified checkpoints in
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
is a superset of the set of justified checkpoints in
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i}
, both
Item

22.5
and
Item

22.6
are proven.

Items

22.1
and
22.2
.

We know that
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are honest-output-dynamically-equivalent up to round
{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}
.
If
t_{i}>0
, then, by the inductive hypothesis,
Item

22.7
implies this, if
t=0
, then this is vacuously true.
Hence, due to
item

(iv)
of
A^{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}
’s set of decisions,
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i}
and
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t_{i})}}_{i}
are dynamically-equivalent.
From this follows that
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
are also honest-output-dynamically-equivalent up to round
{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}
and, therefore,
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
and
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
are dynamically-equivalent as well.
This and
Items

22.5
and
22.6
imply
Items

22.1
and
22.2
.

Item

22.3
.

By
Algorithm

4
,

\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\in\{\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i}-1)}}_{i},\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch},(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i})^{\lceil\kappa}\}
.
Let us consider each case.

Case 1:
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i}-1)}}_{i}
.

This case implies
t_{i}>0
.
Hence, we can apply the inductive hypothesis.
Specifically,
Item

22.4
for slot
t_{i}-1
implies

Item

22.3
for slot
t_{i}
.

Case 2:
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},t_{i}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}

Due to
4
,
4
and
4
,
\mathsf{J}(\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},t_{i}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}})
.
Hence, by following the reasoning applied when discussing
Items

22.5
and
22.6
,
there exists a slot
t_{k}\in[0,t_{i})
and validator
v_{k}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}
such that
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{k})}}_{k}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
.
Therefore, we can apply the inductive hypothesis.
Specifically,
Item

22.3
for slot
t_{k}
implies

Item

22.3
for slot
t_{i}
.

Case 3:
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}=(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i})^{\lceil\kappa}
.

Algorithm

3
of
Algorithm

3
implies that
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\succeq\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
.
Then,

Item

22.2
implies that
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
.

Item

22.4
.

By
4
to
4
,
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}\in\{\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i},\mathcal{GJ}(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}).\mathsf{ch},\mathsf{ch}^{C}\}
with
(\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q)=\texttt{fastconfirm}(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i},t_{i})\land Q\neq\emptyset
.
Let us consider each case.

Case 1:
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{i})}}_{i}
.

In this case,

implies

.

Case 2:
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}).\mathsf{ch}
.

and

, this means that there exists a slot

and validator

.
Then,

for slot
t_{k}
implies

Item

22.4
for slot
t_{i}
.

Case 3:
\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}
with
(\mathsf{ch}^{C},Q)=\texttt{fastconfirm}(\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i},t_{i})\land Q\neq\emptyset
.

This implies that
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}
includes a quorum of
vote
message for chain
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
.
Given

,
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\mathcal{V}}{}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}
also includes a quorum of
vote
message for chain
\mathsf{ch}^{C}
.
Hence,
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}=\mathsf{chAva}^{{\mathsf{fconf}(t_{i})}}_{i}
.

.

As argued in the proof of

and

, we know that
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
and

’s set of decisions clearly imply

up to round

Lemma 14

(Analogous of
Lemma

4
)
.

If, in slot
t
, all validators in

cast

messages for chains extending chain
\mathsf{ch}
,
then, for any validator

\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t+1)}}_{i}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
, which implies that, in slot

, all validators in

cast
vote
messages for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}
.

Proof.

Take any
\eta
-compliant execution

of

4
where in slot

, all validators in

cast

\mathsf{ch}
.

proves to exist.

of execution

as well.

, in execution

and

of

then it follows that this holds for execution

as well, which implies that, in slot
t+1
, all validators in

cast
vote
messages for chains extending
\mathsf{ch}
.
∎

)
.

Lemma 16

(Analogous of
Lemma

7
)
.

Let
r_{i}
be any round and
r_{j}
be any round such that
r_{j}\geq r_{i}
and
r_{j}\in\{{\mathsf{propose}(r_{j})},{\mathsf{vote}(r_{j})}\}
. Then, for any validator
v_{i}
honest in round
r_{i}
and any validator
v_{j}\in H_{r_{j}}
,

\mathsf{chAva}^{r_{i}}_{i}\preceq\mathsf{ch}^{r_{j}}_{j}
.

Proof.

Take any
\eta
-compliant execution
{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}
of
Algorithm

4
.
Let
{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}
be an equivalent execution of
Algorithm

3
which
Lemma

13
proves to exist.
Let
r_{i}
be any round and
r_{j}
be any round such that
r_{j}\geq r_{i}
and
r_{j}\in\{{\mathsf{propose}(r_{j})},{\mathsf{vote}(r_{j})}\}
.
We know that
\mathsf{chAva}^{r_{i}}_{i}
is first set by
v_{i}
in round
r_{i}^{\prime}\leq r_{i}
such that
r_{i}^{\prime}\in\{{\mathsf{vote}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}^{\prime}))},{\mathsf{fconf}(\operatorname{\mathrm{slot}}(r_{i}^{\prime}))}\}
.
From
Items

22.3
and
22.4
of
Lemma

13
, we know that there exits a slot
t_{m}\leq r_{k}
and a validator
v_{m}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}
such that
\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}_{m}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{r_{i}^{\prime}}_{i}=\mathsf{chAva}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.

Lemma

7
implies that
\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{r_{j}}_{j}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}_{m}
.
From
Lemma

13
, we know that
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{r_{j}}_{j}=\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{r_{j}}_{j}
.
Hence,
\overset{{e_{\textsc{ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{r_{j}}_{j}=\overset{{e_{\textsc{no-ffg}}}}{\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}}{}{}^{r_{j}}_{j}\succeq\mathsf{Ch}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{m})}}_{m}\succeq\mathsf{chAva}^{r_{i}^{\prime}}_{i}=\mathsf{chAva}^{r_{i}}_{i}
.
∎

.∎

5.4.1.1
Asynchrony Resilience.

We now move to proving that
Algorithm

4
also satisfies Asynchrony Resilience.
However, compared to
Algorithm

3
, due to the FFG component, to prove Asynchrony Resilience for
Algorithm

4
we also need to require that also
Equation

4
holds.
The reason for this is that we want to avoid a situation where
ffg-vote
s sent by validators in

H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+1)},{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+1)}}\setminus H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}

during the asynchronous period, together with
ffg-vote
s for a slot in
[t_{a}+1,t_{a}+\pi]
but that are sent during a later slot
t_{k}>t_{a}+\pi+2
by validators that are adversarial in
t_{k}
but that are honest in
t_{a}+\pi+2
and are in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a})}}\setminus A_{{\mathsf{vote}(t_{a}+\pi+2)}}
could justify a checkpoint conflicting with either the chain
propose
d or the chain confirmed by an honest validator at or before slot
t_{a}
.

Lemma 18

(Analogous of
Lemma

11
)
.

Assume
\pi>0
and
take a slot
t\leq t_{a}
such that, in slot

\mathsf{ch}
.

casts a

\mathsf{ch}

We proceed by induction on
t_{i}
and add the following condition to the inductive hypothesis.

\mathsf{ch}

,
\left|X^{t^{\prime\prime},\mathsf{ch}}\cup A_{\infty}\right|<\frac{2}{3}n
.

Base Case:

.

From
Lemma

14
which, given that we assume
f<\frac{n}{3}
, also implies
Item

1
.

Inductive Step:
t_{i}>t_{a}
.

We assume that the Lemma holds for any slot

t_{i}
.
Note that

1
holding for slot
t_{i}-1
means that for any validator

and justified checkpoint
\mathcal{J}
according to

,
\mathcal{J}
does not conflict with
\mathsf{ch}
.
Let us proceed by cases and keep in mind that due to
Algorithm

4
, if in slot
t_{i}
a validator

casts a

\mathsf{ch}

\mathsf{ch}

5.4.2
Partial synchrony

In this section, we show that
Algorithm

4
ensures
A
,
B
and
C
and hence ensures that the chain
\mathsf{chFin}
is always Accountably Safe and is live after time
\max(\mathsf{GST},\mathsf{GAT})+{\Delta}
, meaning that
Algorithm

4
is an
\eta
-secure ebb-and-flow protocol under the assumption that
f<\frac{n}{3}
.

Lemma 20
.

Algorithm

4
satisfies
Constraint

A
.

Proof.

Let us prove that Algorithm
4
ensures all the conditions listed in
Constraint

A
.

.

By
Algorithm

4
, an honest active validator sends only one
ffg-vote
in any slot, and therefore,
holds.

.

Direct consequence of
Algorithm

4
.

.

Take any two slots
t
and
t^{\prime}
with
t<t^{\prime}
.
Let
\mathcal{S}_{k}
be the source checkpoint of the
ffg-vote
that validator
v_{i}
sent in any slot
k
.
Now, we prove by induction that
\mathcal{S}_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}\leq\mathcal{S}_{t^{\prime}}
.
To do so, take any two slots
r
and
r^{\prime}
such that
t\leq r<r^{\prime}\leq t^{\prime}
,
v_{i}\in H_{r}
and
r^{\prime}
is the first slot after
r
such that
v_{i}\in H_{r^{\prime}}
.
By
Algorithm

4
, we know that
S_{r}=\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(r)}}_{i}
and by
4
and
4
, we have that
\mathsf{J}(\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(r)}}_{i},\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{vote}(r)}_{i})
.
Then, note that
r\leq r^{\prime}-1
and that because of the joining protocol, we know that
v_{i}
is awake at round
{\mathsf{merge}(r^{\prime}-1)}
.
Hence, we have that
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(r)}}_{i}\leq\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{merge}(r^{\prime}-1)}_{i})=\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(r^{\prime}-1)}}_{i}
.
Finally, by
4
and
4
,
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(r^{\prime}-1)}}_{i}\leq\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(r^{\prime})}}_{i}=\mathcal{S}_{r^{\prime}}
showing that
\mathcal{S}_{r}\leq\mathcal{S}_{r^{\prime}}
.
∎

The proof of
Constraint

B
is trivial so we skip it for now.
Before being able to prove that
Algorithm

4
satisfies
Constraint

C
, we need to show that the base case of
Lemma

6
holds even when
\mathsf{GST}>0
.

Lemma 21
.

Let
t
be a slot with an honest proposer
v_{p}
such that
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}\geq\mathsf{GST}+\Delta
and assume that
v_{p}
casts a
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},\mathcal{GJ}_{p},t,v_{p}]
message.
Then, for any validator
v_{i}\in H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
,
v_{i}
casts a
vote
message for chain
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
and
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
.

Proof.

Suppose that in slot
t
, an honest proposer sends a
[\textsc{propose},\mathsf{ch}_{p},\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p},Q^{C}_{p},\mathcal{GJ}_{p},t,v_{p}]
message.
Consider an honest validator
v_{i}
in
H_{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}
.
Note that due to the synchrony assumption,
\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{p}
.
Hence,
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}\geq\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
.
Now, we want to show that
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.
Let us consider two case.

Case 1:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}\succ\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
.

This implies that at time
{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}
,
v_{i}
has received a quorum of
vote
messages for
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
.
Let us consider three sub cases.

Case 1.1:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}_{p}.\mathsf{ch}
.

Given that
\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}_{p}.\mathsf{ch}
,
4
and
4
set
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.

Case 1.2:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}\succ\mathcal{GJ}_{p}.\mathsf{ch}
.

This implies that
\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{propose}(t)}}_{p}
include a quorum of
vote
messages for
\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.
Due to the synchrony assumption, the quorum of
vote
messages for
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
are in the view of validator
v_{p}
at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
.
Given that
f<\frac{n}{3}
, this case implies that
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
, and therefore

\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.

Case 1.3:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}\nsucceq\mathcal{GJ}_{p}.\mathsf{ch}
.

In this case,
4
to
4
set
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.

Case 2:
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
.

Given that
\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
and
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}\geq\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
,
4
to
4
imply that
\mathsf{ch}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
.

Now, first suppose that at round
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
there exists a chain
\mathsf{ch}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}=\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}
such that
\left|\left(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrm{frozen}}\right)^{\mathsf{ch},t}\cap\mathcal{V}_{i}^{\mathsf{ch},t}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{i},t)\right|}{2}
.
By the Graded Delivery property
[
8
]
, this implies that at time
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
,
\left|\mathcal{V}_{p}^{\mathsf{ch},t}\right|>\frac{\left|\mathsf{S}(\mathcal{V}_{p},t)\right|}{2}
meaning that
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\mathsf{ch}
and hence, in slot
t
,
v_{i}
casts a
vote
message for
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.

If no such a chain exists,
v_{i}
still casts a
vote
for
\mathsf{ch}_{p}
as
\mathsf{ch}_{p}\succeq\mathsf{ch}^{C}_{p}=\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen}}_{i}
.

Also, given that
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}\geq\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
, due to
4
and
4
,
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}=\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
.
∎

Lemma 22
.

Algorithm

4
satisfies
Constraint

C
.

Proof.

Let us prove that
Algorithm

4
ensures each condition listed in
Constraint

C
.

.

Let
[\textsc{vote},\mathsf{ch},\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{T},t,v_{i}]
be the
vote
message cast by validator
v_{i}
in slot
t
.
We need to show that
\mathcal{S}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathcal{T}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathsf{ch}
.

4
,
4
,
4
and
4
imply that
\mathcal{T}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}
and
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}^{{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}\preceq\mathsf{ch}
, hence
\mathcal{T}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathsf{ch}
.

Then, we are left with showing that
\mathcal{S}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathcal{T}.\mathsf{ch}
.
Due to
4
and
4
, this amounts to proving that
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathsf{chAva}^{\mathsf{vote}(t)}_{i}
.
First, note that
Algorithm

4
ensures that
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},4\Delta t}_{i}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}^{\text{frozen},4\Delta t}_{i}
.
This can be proven by induction on slot
t^{\prime}
.
For
t^{\prime}=0
, the base case, this is obvious from the initialization code.
For
t^{\prime}>0
, assume that the statement holds for
t^{\prime}
.
Given that
\texttt{fastconfirm}(\mathcal{V},\cdot)
always outputs a chain descendant of
\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}).\mathsf{ch}
,
4
and
4
ensure that
\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},4\Delta t^{\prime}+3\Delta}_{i}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t^{\prime})}}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
.
Then,
4
,
4
and
4
ensure that this relation is maintained for any round until
{\mathsf{vote}(t^{\prime}+1)}
.

Hence,
\operatorname{\textsc{MFC}}(\cdot,\cdot,\mathsf{ch}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}},\cdot)\succeq\mathcal{GJ}^{\text{frozen},4\Delta t+\Delta}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
.
Therefore,
Algorithm

4
ensures that
\mathsf{chAva}_{i}^{\mathsf{vote}(t)}\succeq\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen},4\Delta t+\Delta}_{i}).\mathsf{ch}
.

.

Due to
4
and
4
, there exists a set
\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{FFGvote},t}_{i}\supseteq\mathcal{V}^{{\mathsf{merge}(t-1)}}_{i}
such that
\mathcal{GJ}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{FFGvote},t}_{i})
.

.

Algorithm

4
implies that any
ffg-vote

\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{T}
sent by an honest validator during slot
t
is such that
\mathcal{T}_{i}.c=t
.

.

Follows from
Lemma

8
.

.

Let the greatest justified checkpoint in the view of
v_{p}
at round
{\mathsf{propose}(t)}
be
\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
and
v_{i}
be any always-honest validator.
By
Lemma

21
,
\mathcal{GJ}^{\mathrm{frozen},{\mathsf{vote}(t)}}_{i}=\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
.
Hence, by
Algorithm

4
, at time
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
, validator
v_{i}
sends an
ffg-vote

\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{T}_{i}
with the same source checkpoint, namely
\mathcal{S}=\mathcal{GJ}_{p}
.
Finally, as argued when proving
, the chain that
v_{i}

vote
s for is a descendant of
\mathcal{T}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}
.
Then, from
Lemma

21
and
Algorithm

4
, we can conclude that
\mathcal{T}_{i}.\mathsf{ch}\preceq\mathsf{ch}_{p}
.

.

Given that
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}\geq\mathsf{GST}
, and that
ffg-vote
s for slot
t
are sent at round
{\mathsf{vote}(t)}
, they are received by any always-honest validator
v_{i}
at time
{\mathsf{merge}(t)}
and, hence, included in
\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t)}}_{i}
.
From
, we then have that
\mathcal{V}^{\text{frozen},{\mathsf{merge}(t)}}_{i}\subseteq\mathcal{V}^{\mathsf{FFGvote},t+1}_{i}
.

.

Let
v_{i}
be any always-honest validator.
By
Lemma

21
, at time
